Batch API is a flat 50% discount with up to 100,000 requests per batch and typical <1h completion, which fits an offline data-build perfectly. Prompt caching makes the shared instruction block ~0.1x cost on reads (1.25x one-time write at 5m TTL).  
  gain: 3-5x lower cost for the same measured data quality vs generating everything on a frontier model; makes the 300-400M-token target reachable on a hobby budget.  
  risk: Haiku 4.5 is a 200K-context model — keep seed documents under ~8K tokens per request. Batch results return in arbitrary order, so key by custom_id, never by position.  
  verdict: (unverified) — 
- [high] **Fix the SFT hyperparameters before generating any new SFT data. In configs/finetune_*.yaml: learning_rate 7.0e-7 → 1.0e-5, min_lr 1.0e-7 → 1.0e-6, batch_size 8 → 32 (micro_batch_size 2, gradient_accumulation 16 = 32,768 tokens/step), max_steps 8,000 → 10,500 (2 epochs over 168K packed sequences), warmup_steps 400 → 300, max_grad_norm 0.5 → 1.0.**  
  why: 7.0e-7 at 8,192 tokens/step is 15-75x below the band implied by SmolLM2's SFT reference (LR 3.0e-4 at ~1.05M tokens/step; sqrt-scaled → 5.3e-5, linear-scaled → 9.4e-6 at 32K tokens/step). At 7e-7 the model is barely updated, which is the simplest explanation for 1.1 finetuning to 41% while 1.0 sits at 43%. The current run also completes only ~0.37 epochs, so most of the 176M-token SFT set is never seen.  
  gain: This alone is plausibly worth more than all synthetic data work — expect the instruction-following and short-writing portions of the benchmark to move several points, and it is a config edit costing one training run (~1-2 hours at 13.5K tok/s for 344M tokens).  
  risk: Higher LR raises catastrophic-forgetting risk on world knowledge (the pattern Thinking Machines measured: midtraining dropped IF-eval 85→79). Pair with recommendation R6 (pretraining replay). Run an A/B at 1e-5 vs 3e-6 on a 500-prompt holdout before committing.  
  verdict: (unverified) — 
- [medium] **Mix 5-10% raw pretraining tokens (loss unmasked) into every SFT shard as a replay/regularization term, i.e. for every 100 packed SFT sequences include 5-10 sequences drawn from data/shards_1.1_repretrain.**  
  why: This is MiniLLM's L_PT auxiliary language-modeling loss, included specifically to preserve general language understanding while optimizing an alignment objective; Thinking Machines' personalization experiment shows the same failure mode (midtraining on domain docs cut IF-eval from 85% to 79%) and the same class of fix. It is what makes the higher SFT learning rate in R5 safe.  
  gain: Prevents the 2-5 point knowledge regression that typically accompanies a 15x LR increase; enables R5's gain to be realized net.  
  risk: Slightly dilutes SFT signal per step; compensate by keeping max_steps at 10,500 rather than shortening the run.  
  verdict: (unverified) — 
- [high] **Delete all hand-written-and-repeated SFT records from scripts/build_balanced_sft.py (essay_records, concise_behavior_records and the equivalent blocks in build_rescue_sft.py / build_focus_sft.py). Replace with generated data under a hard rule: no response text may appear more than once in the corpus, enforced by exact-hash dedup plus MinHash-LSH removal at Jaccard >= 0.7 on 5-gram shingles.**  
  why: Repeating 4-9 gold answers dozens of times teaches verbatim recall of those specific strings, not the behavior. Cosmopedia's team spent most of their project time on prompt engineering precisely to hold duplicate content under 1%, and had to explicitly instruct the generator away from repeated openers. Magpie's result (300K filtered beating 1M unfiltered by 2-3x on AlpacaEval 2 LC) shows the payoff is in diversity, not count.  
  gain: Removes a known regression vector; the repeated-essay pattern is very likely part of why 1.1 produces template-y output.  
  risk: Short-term: hand-written examples currently anchor formatting behavior, so removing them without replacement will regress format compliance. Generate replacements first, then delete.  
  verdict: (unverified) — 
- [high] **Generate 800K-1M raw synthetic instruction-response pairs and filter down to 250-300K (keep rate 25-35%). Two-stage protocol: (a) query generation — one API call produces 40-60 distinct user queries, conditioned on {one random real document} x {one of 25+ personas} x {one of 12 task categories}, temperature 1.0; (b) answer generation — separate call per query, temperature 0.6-0.7, with a length target matched to the query type (20-60 tokens for factual, 100-200 for writing).**  
  why: Magpie's exact numbers: 4M generated (3M Air + 1M Pro), filtered to 300K, and the 300K filtered set produced 25.08% AlpacaEval 2 LC vs 12.89% for the 1M-example OpenHermes 2.5. Magpie's own mechanism (feeding a bare pre-query chat template) is not reproducible against the Claude API, which has no raw-completion endpoint and no assistant prefill on current models — so the document-seeded two-stage variant is the correct substitute, and it is also what phi-4 and OSS-Instruct do.  
  gain: Plasma already has 168K/176M tokens of public instruct data; the gain here is quality and on-distribution style, not count. Expect instruction-following and short-writing to be the benchmark categories that move most, on the order of the ShareGPT → Magpie-Pro delta scaled down for a 521M model.  
  risk: Free-generated queries drift toward a narrow 'assistant-y' distribution. The document seed is the primary defense. Also budget: ~150M output tokens at Haiku batch ≈ $400.  
  verdict: (unverified) — 
- [high] **Apply this exact filtering cascade to all synthetic instruction data, in this order: (1) exact-hash dedup; (2) drop any new instruction whose ROUGE-L against any accepted instruction is >= 0.7; (3) MinHash-LSH dedup on responses at Jaccard >= 0.7 over 5-gram shingles; (4) drop responses containing refusal/meta strings ('As an AI', 'I cannot', 'I'm sorry, but', 'As a language model'); (5) length bounds — drop responses <8 tokens or >768 tokens; (6) difficulty filter — for verifiable items, generate k=3 answers and drop items where all 3 are identical-trivial or where no 2 agree; (7) benchmark decontamination — 10-gram overlap detection then SequenceMatcher, drop the sample if the matched substring exceeds 50% of the benchmark sample's length.**  
  why: Every threshold here is lifted from a published pipeline: ROUGE-L 0.7 from Self-Instruct (whose own audit found ~46% of unfiltered data had problems); min-neighbor-distance/reward filtering from Magpie's 4M→300K reduction; answer-consistency difficulty filtering from phi-4 ('very easy and very hard questions are dropped'); the 10-gram + SequenceMatcher + 50% rule verbatim from Cosmopedia's decontamination.  
  gain: Turns a ~46%-defective raw pool into a ~300K set of the quality that produced Magpie's 2-3x AlpacaEval margin. Also protects the honest benchmark from contamination, which matters because a leaked eval will make the 43%→X comparison meaningless.  
  risk: Over-filtering can collapse diversity (removing everything unusual). Monitor kept-set diversity with distinct-4 n-gram ratio and embedding-space coverage before and after; if keep rate falls below 20% the thresholds are too tight.  
  verdict: (unverified) — 
- [medium] **For code, build a phi-1-style corpus rather than scraping more code: (a) ~150M tokens of synthetic 'code textbook' exposition, each passage seeded on a real Python file from The Stack (do not free-generate); (b) ~80M tokens of exercise/solution pairs in docstring→implementation form; (c) execute every generated solution against generated assert-based tests in a sandbox and keep only passing pairs (expect 50-70% pass rate). Allocate this to 15% of the decay-window token budget and 15-20% of the SFT set.**  
  why: phi-1-small — 350M parameters, the closest published analogue to Plasma — reached 45% pass@1 on HumanEval from 6B tokens of filtered textbook-quality web code plus 1B tokens of GPT-3.5 synthetic textbooks and exercises. Magicoder shows 75K OSS-seeded examples suffice to beat ChatGPT on HumanEval+ at 7B. Execution verification is the step OpenThoughts and Magicoder both rely on and the one that makes generated code trustworthy.  
  gain: Plasma is at ~0% executable code. Because Plasma is general-purpose (not code-only like phi-1-small) and will spend ~15% rather than ~100% of tokens on code, target 8-20% pass@1 on simple/easy problems rather than 45%. That is still the single largest categorical jump available.  
  risk: Highest-cost item and the one needing a sandboxed executor. Verified generation on Sonnet 5 batch for ~80M output tokens ≈ $400 plus retries. Also: heavy code fraction can regress general prose; hold at 15-20%, not more.  
  verdict: (unverified) — 
- [high] **For math and reasoning, generate SHORT worked solutions only. Hard-cap chain-of-thought at 128 tokens; mix 80% short/direct : 20% longer worked solution (Mix Distillation, alpha = 0.2). Explicitly forbid reflective/backtracking tokens ('wait', 'hmm', 'let me reconsider', 'actually') in the generation prompt. Do NOT ingest OpenThoughts-114k, DeepSeek-R1 traces, s1K, or LIMO.**  
  why: Small Model Learnability Gap measured Qwen2.5-0.5B at 14.8 (long CoT) vs 19.5 (short CoT), a -4.7 point penalty, and 1.5B at -7.1 — the penalty is largest exactly in Plasma's size class. The paper's diagnosis is that small models absorb the stylistic tokens rather than the reasoning, and that base (non-instruct) models suffer a larger gap than instruct models. Mix Distillation at 80/20 recovered Qwen2.5-3B from 40.3 (long) / 43.4 (short) to 45.9. Plasma's 1024-token context makes long traces structurally unaffordable anyway.  
  gain: Prevents a 3-7 point regression that ingesting fashionable reasoning datasets would cause, and buys roughly the +2.5 point Mix-Distillation delta over pure short-CoT. Arithmetic should move from 10-15% toward 30-50% on 2-3 digit problems with digit-by-digit worked solutions (the split-digits tokenizer setting already helps here).  
  risk: Under-training reasoning depth: multi-step reasoning will likely remain under ~15% at 521M regardless. Do not promise otherwise in the benchmark writeup.  
  verdict: (unverified) — 
- [high] **Sample k=8 rationales per math/reasoning training question (not 1), keep only those whose final answer matches the reference, and train on all surviving rationales for the same question. For a smaller high-value set of ~5,000 questions, push to k=20-30.**  
  why: This is the single strongest published finding for CoT distillation at 125M-1.3B: SCoTD's rationales-per-instance ablation on OpenBookQA goes 1 → 39.0%, 5 → 40.2%, 10 → 40.6%, 20 → 43.2%, 30 → 44.4% (65.8% with self-consistency), with no further gain at 50. The paper explicitly notes this 'is different from many prior practices that train with one rationale per example.' It requires only generated text — no logits.  
  gain: SCoTD took OPT-1.3B from 21.1% to 64.7% on CSQA and from 22.2% to 57.8% on OpenBookQA, and the method worked down to 125M. For Plasma, expect the largest measured gain of any reasoning intervention available without logits.  
  risk: Cost scales linearly with k. k=8 over 30K questions at ~150 output tokens each = 36M output tokens ≈ $90 on Haiku batch, $180 on Sonnet 5 intro batch — cheap. The real risk is training on wrong-but-confident rationales; the correct-answer filter is mandatory, not optional.  
  verdict: (unverified) — 
- [medium] **Build a FineWeb-Edu-style quality classifier for the portions of the corpus that were never quality-scored (code 0.97B, StackExchange 0.77B, arXiv 0.35B = 2.09B tokens). Score a 300K-document sample with Haiku 4.5 via Batch API on a 0-5 educational-value rubric (~300M input tokens ≈ $150), train a lightweight linear head on sentence embeddings against those labels, then keep documents scoring >= 3 and up-weight >= 4 in the decay window.**  
  why: SmolLM2's ablations show domain-filtering beat synthesis for math: GSM8K went 10% (OpenWebMath) → 14% (InfiMM-WebMath) → ~20% (FineMath4+), a 2x improvement from filtering alone. Nemotron-CC-HQ's classifier-ensemble subset scored +5.6 MMLU over DCLM. Plasma already ships data/quality_classifier.pkl, so the infrastructure exists — this extends it to the domains it does not cover.  
  gain: Comparable in magnitude to the synthetic-data intervention for math/code specifically, at roughly one-sixth the API cost. FineMath-style filtering doubled GSM8K in a controlled ablation.  
  risk: Classifier labels from a single grader model bake in that model's biases; sample the 0-5 rubric labels with a fixed 5-shot prompt and hold out 2K human-spot-checked items. Over-filtering shrinks an already-small corpus — do not go above a 3+ threshold on the first pass.  
  verdict: (unverified) — 
- [high] **Enforce a hard diversity budget in the generation harness: (seed document) x (4 audiences: young child / high-school / undergraduate / expert) x (>=5 formats) x (3 difficulty tiers), with the topic-cluster conditioning applied to only 50% of prompts, plus an explicit banned-opener list in every system prompt ('Once upon a time', 'In the world of', 'In today's fast-paced', 'Sure!', 'Certainly!', 'Great question'). Track distinct-4 n-gram ratio and reject any generation batch whose first-20-token distinct ratio falls below 0.6.**  
  why: Cosmopedia used exactly 112 topic clusters x 4 audiences x 3 formats for up to 12x prompt multiplication, applied topic conditioning only 50% of the time specifically to increase diversity, targeted <1% duplicate content, and reported that the model reused identical story openers until explicitly told not to. BeyondWeb independently found stylistic and topical homogeneity to be the failure mode that multiple transformation prompts exist to prevent.  
  gain: Directly prevents the mode collapse that makes cheap synthetic corpora worthless; this is the difference between Cosmopedia-quality and template junk. Cosmopedia's team stated most of the project's time went into this, not into compute.  
  risk: Adds meaningful prompt-engineering time before any tokens are generated. Budget 3-5 days for prompt design and a 5M-token pilot before committing the full spend.  
  verdict: (unverified) — 
- [medium] **After the improved base + SFT run, add a rejection-sampling round (RFT): sample n=8 completions per prompt from Plasma itself at temperature 0.9 over ~20K prompts with verifiable answers (arithmetic, unit conversions, code with tests, format-constrained instructions), keep only verified-correct completions, dedup, and finetune for one additional epoch at LR 5e-6.**  
  why: RFT/best-of-n is the only on-policy lever available without teacher logits — it approximates the mode-seeking, on-policy regime that GKD showed is what small-capacity students need (on-policy lambda=1 consistently beat supervised; performance improved monotonically as lambda rose past 25%), using self-generated data plus an external verifier instead of teacher probabilities. Reported RFT progression in a 2024 study: SFT 46.3% → RFT 66.8%.  
  gain: On the verifiable subset (arithmetic, format compliance, simple code), expect the largest post-hoc gain available for zero API cost. Gains are log-linear in the number of distinct correct plans found.  
  risk: Strictly phase 2 — RFT does nothing when the model solves ~0% of problems, which is Plasma's current state on code and multi-step reasoning. Run it only after the base and SFT fixes land. Also risks narrowing output diversity; keep the original SFT mixture in the final epoch.  
  verdict: (unverified) — 
- [medium] **Before spending on data, spend two days on throughput: raise micro_batch_size from 2 to 8-12 at seq_len 1024 (keeping tokens/step at 32,768 by dropping gradient_accumulation to 4 or 2-3), try disabling gradient_checkpointing entirely, and add torch.compile. Target >=30,000 tok/s (~125 TFLOP/s).**  
  why: At micro_batch 2 x seq 1024 the GPU sees only 2,048 tokens per forward, which badly underutilizes a 5080; gradient checkpointing costs a further ~30-40% and is likely unnecessary for a 521M model at 1024 context on 16GB. Measured 56 TFLOP/s against ~450 peak implies 2-3x headroom. Plasma's binding constraint is tokens/param (20 vs 22,000 for Gemma 3 270M, 4,400 for SmolLM 135M) — every throughput multiple converts directly into corpus size.  
  gain: A 2.2x throughput gain turns the 9-day / 10.5B-token run into a 9-day / 23B-token run (44 tokens/param). Combined with WRAP-grade data quality (3x effective), that is roughly a 6x effective-token improvement — larger than any single distillation trick on this list.  
  risk: Larger micro-batches may OOM at 16GB without checkpointing; test at micro_batch 4, 8, 12 and keep checkpointing on if 8 does not fit. torch.compile on Windows + Blackwell can be unstable — verify loss curves match for 200 steps before committing.  
  verdict: (unverified) — 
- [medium] **Set explicit, honest targets in the benchmark harness before the run so the result is falsifiable: overall 50-58% (from 43%/41%); factual QA +5-8 points; short writing +5-10 points; instruction following +8-15 points; arithmetic 10-15% → 30-50% on 2-3 digit problems; executable code 0% → 8-20% pass@1 on easy problems; multi-step reasoning 0% → under 15%.**  
  why: Anchored to: Gemma 3 270M-it (a 6T-token distillation product) scoring HellaSwag 37.7, PIQA 66.2, IFEval 51.2 — i.e. instruction following is where a ~300M model is genuinely competent and knowledge is where it is not; phi-1-small's 45% HumanEval at 350M with a 100%-code budget scaled down to a 15-20% code budget; the Small Model Learnability Gap showing multi-step reasoning stays weak below 3B regardless of teacher quality.  
  gain: Prevents a repeat of the 1.1 = 41% surprise by making the expected per-category delta explicit and checkable at mid-run checkpoints (eval_interval 5000).  
  risk: None. If the mid-run checkpoints miss these bands, abort and re-diagnose rather than finishing a 9-day run.  
  verdict: (unverified) — 

## Making Plasma 1.1 (521M, LLaMA-style, 10.5B tokens, RTX 5080 16GB) actually capable at arithmetic, multi-step reasoning, and basic Python — tokenization, data, corpora, SFT, and curriculum
### Findings
- **ROOT CAUSE OF ~0% EXECUTABLE CODE: the Plasma 1.1 tokenizer physically cannot represent Python indentation. `scripts/train_tokenizer.py` never sets `remove_extra_whitespaces=False`, and SentencePiece defaults that flag to True, so every run of spaces collapses to a single space. Verified on the shipped `data/tokenizer_1.1.model`: encode→decode of `'class A:\n    def f(self):\n        if True:\n            return 1\n'` returns `'class A:\n def f(self):\n if True:\n return 1\n'` — all four nesting levels flattened to one space. A generated function containing ANY nested block therefore raises IndentationError.**  
  Executed locally: `compile(sp.decode(sp.encode('def is_even(n):\n    if n % 2 == 0:\n        return True\n    return False\n')))` → `IndentationError: expected an indented block after 'if' statement on line 2`. A flat one-level function (`def double(n):\n    return n*2`) DOES compile, which is exactly the observed signature: ~0% on real HumanEval/MBPP-style tasks (nearly all need a loop or conditional) while trivial one-liners occasionally work. tokenizer_1.0 is worse — it drops newlines entirely (`'class A: def f(self): if True: return 1'`).  
  _src: C:\Users\eb\Downloads\1386.ai\scripts\train_tokenizer.py lines 89-104 (spm.SentencePieceTrainer.train call, no remove_extra_whitespaces arg); verified against C:\Users\eb\Downloads\1386.ai\data\tokenizer_1.1.model with sentencepiece 0.2.1_
- **ZERO-RETRAIN WORKAROUND EXISTS: TAB characters survive the current tokenizer exactly, via byte fallback `<0x09>`. Tab-indented Python round-trips bit-exact through tokenizer_1.1 today. Cost is +31% tokens on indented code (34 tokens vs 26 for a 5-line nested class).**  
  Executed locally: `sp.decode(sp.encode('class A:\n\tdef f(self, n):\n\t\tif n > 0:\n\t\t\treturn n\n\t\treturn 0\n')) == original` → True. Token stream shows `<0x0A>`, `<0x09>`, `<0x09>` repeated per indent level. The 4-space version of the same string round-trips False.  
  _src: Local experiment on data/tokenizer_1.1.model_
- **SECOND, INDEPENDENT INDENTATION DESTROYER in the data path: `src/data/clean.py::clean_text` collapses all non-newline whitespace (`re.sub(r"[^\S\n]+", " ", text)`, line 12) and then strips every single line (`[line.strip() for line in text.split("\n")]`, line 14). Any code passed through this function loses layout before the tokenizer ever sees it.**  
  Direct read of the file. It is currently not imported anywhere in scripts/ or src/ (grep found no callers), so it may be dead code from the 1.0 pipeline — but it must not be reused for code or math (LaTeX alignment also dies).  
  _src: C:\Users\eb\Downloads\1386.ai\src\data\clean.py lines 12-16_
- **THIRD CODE-DAMAGING BUG: `scripts/build_pretrain_corpus.py::text_docs()` treats a blank line as a document boundary and drops fragments under 200 chars (120 for StackExchange). Python files have blank lines between functions by convention (PEP 8), so every code file is shredded into disconnected fragments, each `.strip()`ed (which also kills the first line's indentation of any fragment that began mid-block).**  
  `text_docs()` lines 54-71: `if not line.strip(): ... yield doc`; `doc = "\n".join(buffer).strip()`. `SOURCES["code"] = (RAW/"code_clean.txt", 0.15)` routes through the `else` branch at line 96-97, i.e. straight into `text_docs`.  
  _src: C:\Users\eb\Downloads\1386.ai\scripts\build_pretrain_corpus.py lines 36-42, 54-71, 96-97_
- **The SFT mix contains ZERO code-instruction data. 430k capped examples across SlimOrca 120k, UltraChat 80k, WizardLM 50k, MetaMath 45k, Alpaca 40k, HH-helpful 30k, OASST 25k, Capybara 15k, Dolly 15k, No-Robots 10k. No Magicoder OSS-Instruct, no Evol-CodeAlpaca, no self-oss-instruct, no CodeAlpaca. There is no supervision anywhere that teaches 'write me a Python function'.**  
  `SOURCES` dict at lines 30-41 of the SFT builder; `"synthetic_included": False` in the emitted manifest.  
  _src: C:\Users\eb\Downloads\1386.ai\scripts\build_sft_v2.py lines 30-41, 217_
- **The pretrain corpus contains ~0% dedicated mathematics. Composition is FineWeb-Edu 4.49B (59.7%), code 0.97B (12.9%), Wikipedia 0.93B (12.4%), StackExchange 0.77B (10.2%), arXiv 0.35B (4.7%) = 7.52B unique. arXiv is research math (LaTeX proofs), not grade-school/computational math, and does not transfer to GSM8K-style problems.**  
  SOURCES weights in build_pretrain_corpus.py (fineweb 0.42, wiki 0.20, code 0.15, stackexchange 0.13, arxiv 0.10) plus the stated corpus token counts. No OpenWebMath, FineMath, InfiMM-WebMath, or MegaMath anywhere in the repo.  
  _src: C:\Users\eb\Downloads\1386.ai\scripts\build_pretrain_corpus.py lines 36-42_
- **Digit tokenization in Plasma is already CORRECT and this lever is exhausted. Verified: '123 + 456 = 579' → ['▁','1','2','3','▁+','▁','4','5','6','▁=','▁','5','7','9']; '1,234,567' → digits and commas all separate; '3.14159' → digit-per-token. Single-digit tokenization is the empirically best scheme for arithmetic; there is nothing left to gain from re-tokenizing numbers.**  
  Local encode test on tokenizer_1.1.model. External confirmation: controlled from-scratch comparisons find 'single-digit tokenization consistently yields better arithmetic performance' and that 'left-to-right 3-digit chunking undermines arithmetic accuracy'.  
  _src: Local test; https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.14903 (Singh & Strouse, 'Tokenization counts', ICLR 2025); https://arxiv.org/html/2510.06824v2 (BitTokens, 2025)_
- **Right-to-left vs left-to-right integer tokenization is worth 22-72 accuracy points on frontier models — but ONLY for models with multi-digit tokens. Measured: GPT-3.5-Turbo 7-9 digit addition 50.3%→97.0% (+46.7), 17-19 digit 35.3%→85.0% (+49.7); Claude 2.1 28.3%→100% and 0.3%→72.7%; GPT-4 90.6%→100%. Llama-3 uses L2R 3-digit tokens (all 1-3 digit numbers have dedicated IDs) and pays this tax; Claude 3+ uses R2L. Plasma's split_digits=True sidesteps the whole issue.**  
  Beren Millidge's R2L measurements plus Singh & Strouse: 'right-to-left tokenization (enforced by comma separating numbers at inference time) leads to largely improved performance'; 'the gap between tokenization directions decreases when models are scaled'. Singh & Strouse report >22pp average improvement.  
  _src: https://www.beren.io/2024-07-07-Right-to-Left-Integer-Tokenization/ ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.14903_
- **Exotic number embeddings (xVal, FoNE, Abacus) are NOT the right investment for Plasma. BitTokens (2025) trained nanoGPT models from scratch on FineWeb-10B + 30M arithmetic samples per task and found: single-digit beats subword; xVal 'performs poorly on all tasks due to limited precision'; FoNE learns addition but 'struggles with multiplication and division' — and FoNE provably requires decode→compute→re-encode for multiplication (Proposition 4.3). Abacus embeddings do reach 99% on 100-digit addition after training on 20 digits (6x length generalization) but are a positional-embedding surgery on a pure-arithmetic model, not a general LM.**  
  BitTokens ablation table; Abacus paper: 122M-param transformer, 20M addition samples, 24 GPU-hours on one A4000, 99% at 100 digits and >95% at 120 digits, vs FIRE's 2.5x ceiling. Index hints (Zhou et al.) were seed-unstable: 'varying from near perfect to 0% accuracy'.  
  _src: https://arxiv.org/html/2510.06824v2 ; https://arxiv.org/html/2405.17399 (McLeish et al., NeurIPS 2024)_
- **Synthetic arithmetic data DOES generalize when the output format carries the algorithm, and the sample counts are small enough to be trivially affordable. Goat: LLaMA-7B + LoRA on ~1M synthetically generated Q/A pairs (numbers sampled log-uniformly across magnitudes) reaches BIG-bench arithmetic 97.8% on 8D+8D addition, 98.4% on 5D-5D subtraction, 97.8% on 3Dx3D multiplication and 96.5% on 5D÷5D — vs GPT-4's 92.1 / 92.4 / 30.3 / 53.4. Critically, addition and subtraction are learnable END-TO-END with no scratchpad; multiplication and division are NOT and require explicit task decomposition (distributive expansion for ×, iterative long division for ÷). Training cost: ~1.5 hours on a 24GB GPU for 100K instances.**  
  Goat paper: 1M samples, 1 epoch, batch 128, LR 3e-4, LoRA rank 64/alpha 64. The paper explicitly attributes its success to 'LLaMA's consistent tokenization of numbers' (digit-per-token) — the same property Plasma already has.  
  _src: https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.14201 / https://ar5iv.labs.arxiv.org/html/2305.14201 (Goat, Liu & Low 2023)_
- **At ~100M params, arithmetic needs only 120k-300k samples if the positional/format representation is right. 'Positional Description Matters for Transformer Arithmetic' trains a 100M-param model on 300k samples and gets essentially perfect 12-digit and strong 15-digit multiplication with NO scratchpad; addition needs 'a mere 120k samples' and extrapolates 10→12 digits. In natural-language context (word problems), the same setup reaches near-perfect 5-digit addition where the baseline caps at 3 digits (i.e. pure memorization at 120k samples).**  
  Paper abstract/method: modifications to positional encoding and to the representation of the arithmetic task (zero-padding, explicit position markers, LSB-first ordering). Plasma is 5x larger than this model, so the sample requirement is an upper bound.  
  _src: https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.14737 (Shen et al., Microsoft Research 2023)_
- **TinyGSM is the single most important existence proof for this project: a 125M-parameter model scores 63.1% on GSM8K. Full table (pass@1, no verifier): 125M → 63.1%, 350M → 65.9%, 1.3B → 68.2%, 2.7B → 74.3%. With a same-size verifier (verify48@1): 68.9% / 71.3% / 81.5%. The dataset is 12.3M GSM8K-style word problems / 1.8B tokens, generated by GPT-3.5-turbo from the 7.4k original GSM8K training questions, cost ~$3,600. The solutions are PYTHON PROGRAMS, not natural-language CoT — this deliberately 'circumvents language models' known limitation regarding numerical calculations' by offloading arithmetic to the interpreter. Data scaling shows diminishing returns: 1/3→2/3 of TinyGSM gives +10%, the final third is marginal.**  
  TinyGSM tables; base models were Phi-1.5-tiny (125M), Phi-1.5-small (350M), Phi-1.5 (1.3B), Phi-2 (2.7B). Scaling the verifier 125M→1.3B alone is worth +7.2 points.  
  _src: https://arxiv.org/abs/2312.09241 (Liu, Bubeck et al., CMU + Microsoft Research)_

### Recommendations
- [high] **FIX THE TOKENIZER WHITESPACE BUG — this is the #1 action and it alone explains ~0% executable code. Two paths. (A) SHIP-NOW, no retokenize, no restart: canonicalize ALL Python indentation to TAB characters in every code shard, every math-code shard, and every code SFT example (`re.sub(r'^( +)', lambda m: '\t'*(len(m.group(1))//4), line, flags=re.M)`), and post-process generations with `.replace('\t','    ')` in src/inference/generate.py and web/model_manager.py before display or `exec`. Verified today: tab-indented Python round-trips bit-exact through tokenizer_1.1 via byte fallback `<0x09>`; 4-space does not. Cost: +31% tokens on indented code (34 vs 26 on a 5-line nested class). (B) FOR PLASMA 1.2: retrain the tokenizer with `remove_extra_whitespaces=False, allow_whitespace_only_pieces=True, user_defined_symbols=['\t','  ','    ','      ','        ','            ','\n']` added to the existing `byte_fallback=True, split_digits=True, normalization_rule_name='identity'`.**  
  why: Verified locally: `compile(sp.decode(sp.encode('def is_even(n):\n    if n % 2 == 0:\n        return True\n    return False')))` raises IndentationError, because SentencePiece's default `remove_extra_whitespaces=True` collapses every indent run to one space. A flat one-level function still compiles, which exactly matches the observed 'occasionally works on trivial prompts, 0% on anything real' signature. No amount of extra code data fixes a model that cannot emit an indent.  
  gain: Moves executable-code pass rate from a mechanical hard 0% to whatever the data supports. On its own (with existing 0.97B code tokens and no code SFT) expect ~1-3% HumanEval / ~3-6% MBPP; combined with recs 4 and 8, 4-9% HumanEval and 9-16% MBPP.  
  risk: Path A: +31% token cost on code shards; requires the tab→spaces post-process to be applied in ALL three inference paths (chat.py, generate.py, web/model_manager.py) or output looks wrong to users. Path B: requires a full pretrain restart — a ~9-day run. Do A now, B in 1.2.  
  verdict: (unverified) — 
- [high] **Stop routing code through prose cleaners. Add a `preserve_layout=True` branch to `src/data/clean.py::clean_text` that skips lines 12 and 14 (`re.sub(r'[^\S\n]+',' ')` and `[line.strip() ...]`), and never call the prose path on code, LaTeX, or math. In `scripts/build_pretrain_corpus.py`, replace `text_docs()` for the `code` source with a JSONL or explicit `<|file_sep|>`-delimited reader so blank lines inside a Python file are not document boundaries, and drop the `.strip()` on the joined doc for code.**  
  why: Two more independent indentation destroyers sit upstream of the tokenizer: clean.py collapses whitespace and strips every line; build_pretrain_corpus.py::text_docs() splits documents on blank lines with min_chars=200, shredding PEP-8-formatted Python into fragments that begin mid-block and then get .strip()ed.  
  gain: Prevents rec 1 from being silently undone. Also recovers coherent multi-function file context, which is what teaches import/def/return structure.  
  risk: Low. Changes shard contents, so shards must be rebuilt (~hours, not days).  
  verdict: (unverified) — 
- [high] **Rebalance the pretrain mix from {fineweb 0.42, wiki 0.20, code 0.15, stackexchange 0.13, arxiv 0.10} to {fineweb 0.36, code 0.23, math 0.10, wiki 0.14, stackexchange 0.12, arxiv 0.05}. On a 10.5B-token seen budget that is ~2.4B code tokens (from Stack-Edu Python/JS/Java rather than raw Stack) and ~1.05B math tokens (FineMath-4+ 9.6B available — subsample 0.8B; plus OpenWebMath 0.25B).**  
  why: Cohere's 470M-2.8B ablations — the closest published scale to Plasma — put the optimum for non-code ability at 25% code; Plasma is at 12.9%. 100% code costs -18.3% NL reasoning and -86% world knowledge, 75% costs -31% world knowledge, so 23-25% is the safe shoulder. Math is currently ~0% dedicated; SmolLM2 runs 5-14%, OLMo 2 midtrain 10.8-20.8%, Qwen2.5-Coder 10%. Stack-Edu's educational filter is worth Python MultiPL-E 20.7→25.6 for free.  
  gain: +3-8% relative on NL reasoning per Cohere; the math slice is the prerequisite for any nonzero GSM8K. Expect MMLU/ARC roughly flat to slightly up, world knowledge (TriviaQA-style) down 2-4% — an acceptable trade given the stated goal.  
  risk: Reducing FineWeb-Edu from 42% to 36% and Wikipedia 20%→14% will cost a little factual QA, which is currently one of Plasma's stronger categories at 43%. Keep wiki ≥14%; do not go below.  
  verdict: (unverified) — 
- [high] **Replace the cosine schedule with Warmup-Stable-Decay (WSD) and add an explicit annealing stage. Concrete: warmup 3,000 steps → constant 1.2e-4 through step 288,000 (90%) → linear decay to 0 over steps 288,000-320,000 (32,000 steps ≈ 1.05B tokens = 10% of budget). Point the annealing stage at a separate shard dir with mixture {FineMath-4+ 22%, TinyGSM-MIND / MathCoder2 12%, Stack-Edu Python + Magicoder-style code 24%, synthetic arithmetic CoT 4%, GSM8K-style word problems 4%, original web/wiki mix 34%}. Requires a `shard_dir_anneal` + `anneal_start_step` field in pretrain_1.1_v2.yaml and a two-phase scheduler in src/train/train.py.**  
  why: This is the single highest-ROI training change and it is replicated three times independently. Llama 3: LR→0 over the final 40B tokens with 30% weight on new high-quality math/code data gave the 8B model +24.0% GSM8K and +6.4% MATH — and was NEGLIGIBLE for 405B, i.e. the technique is strongest exactly at small scale. MiniCPM: decay over 10% of tokens with high-quality data vs pretrain-only gave GSM8K 42.3 vs 27.7, MMLU 50.9 vs 44.6, MBPP 30.3 vs 24.4. Cohere's 40B-token / 20%-code cooldown at 470M-2.8B gave code +20% relative and win-rate +52.3%.  
  gain: Based on MiniCPM's controlled decay ablation (+14.6 GSM8K, +6.3 MMLU, +5.9 MBPP, +2.7 HumanEval) and Llama-3-8B's +24.0% GSM8K, expect +4-10 points on the composite benchmark and the bulk of whatever math/code gain is achievable at this budget. This is where the 43% vs 41% deadlock gets broken.  
  risk: If the run is already deep into cosine, do NOT re-heat the LR (loss spike risk). Instead run a separate continued-pretrain anneal from the final checkpoint: 1.0-1.5B tokens at constant-then-linear LR 3e-5 → 0 on the annealing mixture. That captures most of the effect with zero risk to the main run. Also: 13-gram decontaminate the annealing mix against GSM8K/MATH/MMLU/ARC test sets exactly as FineMath did, or the gains are fake.  
  verdict: (unverified) — 
- [high] **Build a synthetic arithmetic dataset with these exact specs: 2.0M addition/subtraction samples with operands sampled LOG-UNIFORMLY over 1-8 digits (not uniformly — this is Goat's key sampling trick); 1.0M multiplication samples of nD x 1-3D using the Goat distributive decomposition CoT (`397 x 4429 = 4429 x (300+90+7) = 4429x300 + 4429x90 + 4429x7 = 1328700 + 398610 + 31003 = 1758313`); 0.5M division samples with iterative long-division CoT. Strip thousands separators so digit strings stay contiguous. Total ~60-100M tokens = 0.6-1.0% of the 10.5B budget. Put ~70% into the annealing mix and ~30% into SFT.**  
  why: Goat proved 1M synthetic samples on a digit-split tokenizer (which Plasma already has) yields 97.8% on 8D+8D addition, 98.4% on 5D-5D subtraction, 97.8% on 3Dx3D multiplication and 96.5% on 5D÷5D — beating GPT-4's 30.3% on 3Dx3D. Critically, +/- are learnable END-TO-END with no scratchpad, while x and ÷ are NOT and mathematically require decomposition. 'Positional Description Matters' hit near-perfect 12-digit multiplication with only 300k samples at 100M params, and 5-digit addition in natural-language context with 120k samples — Plasma is 5x that size, so these are upper bounds.  
  gain: 2-digit arithmetic from 10-15% to >95%. 3-5 digit addition/subtraction 85-97%. 3-digit x 3-digit multiplication 60-90% WITH the decomposition CoT emitted, near-0% without it. This is the cheapest single benchmark win available.  
  risk: Over-weighting arithmetic degrades language quality — Cohere-style domination effects start showing above ~2-3% of the mix. Cap at 1.0%. Also: without reversed/LSB-first ordering or explicit column CoT, addition beyond ~6 digits will not length-generalize (Abacus needed dedicated positional embeddings to reach 100 digits). Do not promise >8-digit generalization.  
  verdict: (unverified) — 
- [medium] **For GSM8K-style word problems, train the model to emit a PYTHON PROGRAM, not natural-language chain-of-thought, and execute it. Use `allenai/dolmino-mix-1124` → TinyGSM-MIND (6.48B tokens, 17M docs) subsampled to ~400-800M tokens for the annealing stage, plus GSM_MIND (17.1M tokens), GSM8K-synth (539K), BasicMathMJ (11.1M, 664K docs), metamath-owmfilter (84.2M) and personahub_math_v5 (191M) for SFT. Target output format: `def solution():\n\ttotal = 5 * 3\n\treturn total` (tabs, per rec 1). Add an execute-and-return path to src/inference/chat.py.**  
  why: TinyGSM is the existence proof: a 125M-param model hits 63.1% GSM8K pass@1, 350M hits 65.9%, 1.3B hits 68.2% — all by generating Python rather than doing mental arithmetic. This deliberately 'circumvents language models' known limitation regarding numerical calculations'. MegaMath independently confirms the format effect: text-code interleaved math scores 28.1 PAL vs 19.5 for raw code. StarCoder2-3B scores GSM8K 27.7 via PAL with no math SFT at all. Meanwhile natural-language CoT at this scale is hopeless: SmolLM2-360M gets 3.2 after 4 TRILLION tokens.  
  gain: GSM8K natural-language CoT: 0-3% (do not chase this). GSM8K with PAL/program format after 400-800M tokens of TinyGSM-MIND in annealing + 200k program-form SFT examples: realistically 18-40%. TinyGSM's 63% at 125M came from a Phi-1.5 base pretrained on ~150B tokens of textbook-quality synthetic data and 1.8B tokens of task data — Plasma has ~7% of that pretraining budget, so discount accordingly. TinyGSM also shows diminishing returns after 2/3 of the data, so 800M tokens captures most of it.  
  risk: This is task-format specialization, not general reasoning — be honest about it in the benchmark writeup and report both 'GSM8K (CoT)' and 'GSM8K (PAL)' separately. Requires a sandboxed exec path in the inference stack (timeout + restricted namespace; benchmark_v2.py's run_code already has the right shape with a 3s watchdog). Also mandatory: 13-gram decontaminate TinyGSM-MIND against the GSM8K test set.  
  verdict: (unverified) — 
- [high] **Add code-instruction data to the SFT mix, which currently has literally none. Concrete additions to the SOURCES dict in scripts/build_sft_v2.py: `instruct_magicoder_oss: 60000` (ise-uiuc/Magicoder-OSS-Instruct-75K), `instruct_self_oss_sc2: 40000` (bigcode/self-oss-instruct-sc2-exec-filter-50k — the exact set SmolLM2 used), `instruct_evol_codealpaca: 40000` (theblackcat102/evol-codealpaca-v1), `instruct_codealpaca: 15000`. Also raise `instruct_metamath` from 45000 to 80000. Rebalance by trimming SlimOrca 120000→95000 and UltraChat 80000→65000 so total stays ~500k examples. Target ~22% code-instruct, ~16% math-instruct of the SFT mix.**  
  why: The current SFT mix (SlimOrca, UltraChat, WizardLM, MetaMath, Alpaca, HH, OASST, Capybara, Dolly, No-Robots) contains zero examples of 'write a Python function that…'. There is no supervision anywhere in the pipeline for the exact capability being measured. SmolLM2's instruct model gained HumanEval 22.6→28.1 and GSM8K 31.1→48.8 over its base largely from this kind of SFT data. Note Magicoder-OSS-Instruct is exec-filtered, which matters more than volume at this scale.  
  gain: HumanEval from ~0 to 4-9%; MBPP from ~0 to 9-16% (MBPP runs 1.5-1.9x HumanEval at sub-1B scale: Qwen2.5-Coder-0.5B is 28.0/52.9, SantaCoder 18.0/35.0). Instruction-following category should also rise since code prompts are highly structured.  
  risk: Do not expect Qwen2.5-Coder-0.5B numbers (HumanEval 28.0) — that model saw 5.2T tokens at 70% code. SantaCoder needed 236B code tokens for HumanEval 18.0; Plasma will have ~2.4B. SFT cannot manufacture a capability the pretrain never installed. Also, >25% code SFT starts eating conversational quality.  
  verdict: (unverified) — 
- [low] **Raise the SFT learning rate from 1.0e-5 to 2.0e-5 (min_lr 2.0e-6) in configs/finetune_1.1_v2.yaml, and raise max_grad_norm from 0.5 to 1.0. Keep 3 epochs / 16,000 steps.**  
  why: 1.0e-5 with grad-clip 0.5 is a 7B-scale recipe applied to a 521M model. Small models need proportionally larger SFT LRs to actually move; format-learning tasks (emit a Python function, emit a scratchpad) are exactly what a too-low LR fails to install in 3 epochs.  
  gain: Faster/complete format acquisition for the new code and program-form math SFT data. Modest on its own (~1-2 points composite), meaningful as an enabler of recs 6 and 7.  
  risk: Higher LR on a 521M model can induce repetition/degeneration if the base is undertrained. Run 2,000 steps at 2.0e-5 and compare held-out assistant-token loss against the 1.0e-5 baseline before committing to the full 16,000. If loss is worse, fall back to 1.5e-5. Lower confidence than the data recommendations.  
  verdict: (unverified) — 
- [high] **Fix the eval harness before measuring any of this: in scripts/benchmark_v2.py raise `gen(..., max_tokens=160)` to 512 for math and reasoning, and the code budget from 220 to 512. Add separate reporting lines for 'arithmetic (direct)', 'arithmetic (CoT)', 'GSM8K (natural-language CoT)' and 'GSM8K (PAL/program)'. Also apply the tab→4-space conversion (rec 1) inside `run_code` before `exec`.**  
  why: Column-wise addition CoT on 8-digit operands is 150-250 tokens with digit-per-token tokenization; a Goat multiplication decomposition is 200-400. At max_tokens=160 every scratchpad answer gets truncated mid-computation and scores 0, so the training improvement would be invisible. And without the tab conversion, rec 1's output will fail `compile()` in the harness even though it is correct.  
  gain: Correct attribution — prevents discarding a working intervention because the measurement truncated it. Zero model cost.  
  risk: Longer generations slow the benchmark ~3x. Also note seq_len is 1024, so a long scratchpad plus prompt can approach the context limit on multi-step problems — worth checking the prompt+completion length distribution.  
  verdict: (unverified) — 
- [high] **Do NOT change number tokenization, and do NOT add Abacus/FoNE/xVal number embeddings. Keep `split_digits=True, byte_fallback=True, normalization_rule_name='identity'` exactly as-is. Invest that effort in output FORMAT instead: LSB-first (reversed) intermediate results inside scratchpads, and explicit column-by-column addition steps.**  
  why: Verified locally that Plasma already emits one token per digit with separators split ('1,234,567' → ▁,1,',',2,3,4,',',5,6,7). Single-digit is the empirically best scheme; the entire 22-72-point right-to-left literature applies only to models with multi-digit tokens (GPT-3.5 50.3→97.0%, Claude 2.1 28.3→100%) and is therefore already banked. BitTokens' from-scratch ablation found xVal 'performs poorly on all tasks' and FoNE 'struggles with multiplication and division' with a proven structural limitation (multiplication requires decode→compute→re-encode). Abacus works but is positional-embedding surgery validated only on pure-arithmetic models, not general LMs.  
  gain: Avoids burning a full retokenize + pretrain restart (~9 days) on a lever that is already pulled. Reversed-order/scratchpad formatting is the remaining representational gain and costs only data-generation time.  
  risk: None — this is a 'don't do the expensive thing' recommendation.  
  verdict: (unverified) — 
- [high] **Keep the 26-layer / 1280-dim shape. Do not trade depth for width to buy throughput. If a throughput/parameter trade is needed, take it from vocab (48k → 32k), intermediate_size (3584 → 3328), or heads — never from num_layers.**  
  why: 'Physics of Language Models Part 2.1' shows a 4-layer/1920-dim transformer underperforms a 20-layer/576-dim one on grade-school math, and a 16-layer/576-dim beats a 4-layer/1920-dim at HALF the parameters. Reasoning depth in these models is literally layer-by-layer recursive dependency resolution — shallow layers resolve dependencies near the query, deep layers resolve distant ones. At 26 layers Plasma is deeper than every configuration in that study, which reached 99% on iGSM-med (op≤15) with OOD generalization to op 20-23. Architecture is not the bottleneck; data is.  
  gain: Preserves the one architectural property that actually predicts multi-step reasoning ability at this scale.  
  risk: 26 layers at hidden 1280 with gradient checkpointing is part of why throughput is 13.5k tok/s. Accept it — the throughput headroom should come from micro_batch/flash-attention/torch.compile, not from layer count.  
  verdict: (unverified) — 
- [medium] **Set these as the honest target numbers for Plasma 1.1 after all fixes, and publish them alongside 1.0's 43% / old-1.1's 41%: HumanEval pass@1 4-9%; MBPP pass@1 9-16%; 2-digit arithmetic >95%; 5-digit addition 80-95%; 3x3-digit multiplication 55-85% (with decomposition CoT); GSM8K natural-language CoT 2-6%; GSM8K program/PAL form 18-40%; MATH <2%. Do not target MATH.**  
  why: Derived from the closest published comparables at matched or larger budgets: SantaCoder 1.1B / 236B code tokens → HumanEval 18.0, MBPP 35.0; Qwen2.5-Coder-0.5B / 5.2T tokens → 28.0 / 52.9; SmolLM2-360M / 4T tokens → GSM8K 3.2; SmolLM2-1.7B / 11T → GSM8K 31.1, HumanEval 22.6. Plasma will have ~2.4B code tokens and ~1.05B math tokens against a 10.5B total. Goat's 97.8% on 8D+8D and TinyGSM's 63.1% at 125M set the ceilings for the task-specific paths.  
  gain: Composite benchmark from 41% to roughly 50-57%, with the code and arithmetic categories carrying almost all of the delta. This is what decisively beating 1.0's 43% looks like.  
  risk: MATH at <2% and natural-language GSM8K at 2-6% will look bad in a table. Report them anyway and report the PAL number next to them — hiding the CoT number and showing only PAL is the kind of thing that gets caught.  
  verdict: (unverified) — 
- [high] **Run a cheap, decisive ablation BEFORE committing 9 days: take the existing 1.1_v2 checkpoint and do three 300M-token continued-pretrain runs at LR 3e-5→0 (~9,000 steps each, ~6 hours each at 13.5k tok/s): (A) current mixture (control), (B) +FineMath-4+ at 14% + Stack-Edu at 24%, (C) B plus tab-normalized code and 60M tokens of synthetic arithmetic CoT. Evaluate all three with the fixed harness (rec 9).**  
  why: Every recommendation above is drawn from other people's ablations at other scales. 18 GPU-hours buys a direct measurement on Plasma's own corpus, tokenizer and eval before spending a week. This mirrors exactly how SmolLM2 (Llama-3.2-3B continued-pretrain ablations for FineMath) and MegaMath (55B-token controlled budget) validated their mixes.  
  gain: De-risks the full run. Expect run C to show the code-execution rate move off zero immediately — that single binary observation validates or kills the tokenizer diagnosis in 6 hours.  
  risk: Continued-pretrain deltas at 300M tokens understate what a proper 1.05B-token anneal delivers, so treat run C's numbers as a lower bound, not a forecast.  
  verdict: (unverified) — 
- [high] **Add 13-gram decontamination against the GSM8K, MATH, MMLU, ARC, HumanEval and MBPP test sets to every new data source (FineMath, TinyGSM-MIND, MathCoder2, Magicoder, Stack-Edu). The existing SFT builder already has a `decontam_dropped` counter — extend the same mechanism to the pretrain and annealing shard builders, which currently have none.**  
  why: FineMath used exactly this (13-gram overlap vs GSM8K/MATH/MMLU/ARC), and TinyGSM-MIND is derived from GSM8K's own training questions, so contamination risk against the test set is real. Every gain claimed above is worthless if it is memorization — and the project's stated standard is honest evaluation.  
  gain: Credibility of the entire benchmark comparison against 1.0. Possibly a small honest reduction in reported scores, which is the point.  
  risk: 13-gram matching over 10B tokens is CPU-expensive; use a Bloom filter over test-set 13-grams (a few hundred MB) rather than exact sets. Budget ~2-4 hours of CPU on the full corpus.  
  verdict: (unverified) — 

## SFT recipe for a 521M from-scratch LLaMA-style base (Plasma 1.1) on a single 16GB RTX 5080: LR/epochs, packing, loss masking, EOS/stopping, chat template, data scale and mixture
### Findings
- **Plasma's planned SFT LR of 1.0e-5 is 5-10x too low for a 521M model. The two best-documented sub-2B SFT recipes use 1.0e-3 (SmolLM2-135M/360M) and 3.0e-4 (SmolLM2-1.7B), both cosine with warmup_ratio 0.1, 2 epochs, max_seq_length 8192, effective batch 128 sequences (per_device 4 x grad_accum 4 x 8 GPUs).**  
  recipes/smollm2/sft/config_smol.yaml (used for BOTH 135M and 360M): `learning_rate: 1.0e-03 # 3e-4`, `lr_scheduler_type: cosine`, `num_train_epochs: 2`, `warmup_ratio: 0.1`, `max_seq_length: 8192`, `per_device_train_batch_size: 4`, `gradient_accumulation_steps: 4`, dataset `HuggingFaceTB/smol-smoltalk`. The 1.7B config is identical except `learning_rate: 3.0e-04` and dataset `HuggingFaceTB/smoltalk`. README confirms 8 GPUs with zero3.  
  _src: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/huggingface/alignment-handbook/main/recipes/smollm2/sft/config_smol.yaml , https://raw.githubusercontent.com/huggingface/alignment-handbook/main/recipes/smollm2/sft/config.yaml , https://raw.githubusercontent.com/huggingface/alignment-handbook/main/recipes/smollm2/README.md_
- **The closest apples-to-apples LR evidence: SmolTulu ran the exact same base model and the exact same Tulu-3 SFT mixture at two LR/batch settings and got a 13.2-point IFEval swing from LR alone. LR 9.0e-5 @ batch 8 beat LR 3.1e-6 @ batch 32 on IFEval 61.0 vs 47.8 and GSM8K 49.0 vs 42.8. Tulu-3 at 4096 max_len means batch 8 = at most 32,768 tokens/step, which is EXACTLY Plasma's tokens-per-optimizer-step.**  
  SmolTulu Table 2: SFT-1130 LR 9.0e-5 / BS 8 / ratio 11.25e-6 vs SFT-1207 LR 3.1e-6 / BS 32 / ratio 0.097e-6. Results: ARC 51.0 vs 55.6, GSM8K 49.0 vs 42.8, IFEval 61.0 vs 47.8, HellaSwag 61.5 vs 67.5, PIQA 72.7 vs 76.9. Tulu 3 8B ratio = 0.039e-6, 70B = 0.016e-6. Paper concludes 'smaller models may require substantially different optimization dynamics than their larger counterparts' and that higher LR/BS ratios win specifically on reasoning tasks (ARC, GSM8K, IFEval).  
  _src: https://arxiv.org/html/2412.08347v1 (SmolTulu: Higher Learning Rate to Batch Size Ratios)_
- **The '1e-5 to 2e-5 Llama-2 SFT range' comment in configs/finetune_1.1.yaml is a category error: those numbers come from 7B-70B models at 64-128 sequence batches. Tulu 3 uses 5e-6 for 8B and 2e-6 for 70B at effective batch 128 x 4096 = 524,288 tokens/step. TRL's own generic SFT default is 2e-5. LR must scale UP as model size goes down and batch goes down.**  
  Tulu 3 8B model card: 'Learning Rate 5E-6 (8B)', 'Effective Batch Size: 128', 'Max Sequence Length 4096', 'Epochs 2', 'Warmup ratio 0.03', 'Learning Rate Schedule Linear', 'Loss Accumulation: Sum'. TRL docs: SFTConfig `learning_rate` 'Defaults to 2e-5 instead of 5e-5'.  
  _src: https://huggingface.co/allenai/Llama-3.1-Tulu-3-8B-SFT , https://huggingface.co/docs/trl/sft_trainer_
- **2 epochs is the modern standard for SFT sets of this size, not 3. Both SmolLM2 (1.1M samples full / 485k smol subset) and Tulu 3 (939,344 samples) train exactly 2 epochs. 15 epochs is only for LIMA-scale (1,030 example) sets.**  
  SmolLM2 configs: `num_train_epochs: 2`. SmolTalk card: 'trained using 2 epochs, learning rate 3e-04, sequence length 8192, global batch size 16'. Tulu 3 8B card: 'Epochs 2'. LIMA: 'fine-tune for 15 epochs using AdamW with beta1=0.9, beta2=0.95, weight decay 0.1. Without warmup steps, initial learning rate 1e-5 linearly decaying to 1e-6', batch 32 examples, on 1,000 examples.  
  _src: https://huggingface.co/datasets/HuggingFaceTB/smoltalk , https://huggingface.co/allenai/Llama-3.1-Tulu-3-8B-SFT , https://ar5iv.labs.arxiv.org/html/2305.11206_
- **MEASURED BUG in the current pipeline: scripts/build_sft_v2.py uses 'wrapped' packing (np.concatenate of every conversation, then reshape to 1025-token rows). With 423,235 conversations packed into 171,978 rows, approximately 171,977 row boundaries each fall inside a conversation, so ~40.6% of all conversations are split across a row boundary. Every split produces (a) a head fragment with no EOS and (b) a tail fragment where the model is supervised to continue an assistant answer with no question in context.**  
  data/sft_shards_1.1_v2/meta.yaml: n_train_sequences 168539, n_val_sequences 3439 (=171,978 rows), total_tokens 176,278,465, source_counts sum = 423,235 conversations, mean 417 tokens/conversation. Code: `all_tokens = np.concatenate(conv_tokens); n_seq = len(all_tokens) // PACK_LEN; packed = all_tokens[:trim].reshape(n_seq, PACK_LEN)`. 171,977 boundaries / 423,235 conversations = 40.6%.

- **MEASURED: 11.1% of the SFT conversations exceed the 1024-token context and can never fit in one row, dominated by UltraChat (48.8% over 1024) and Capybara (50.0% over 1024). Length distribution over a 40,000-conversation sample: median 257, mean 417, p90 1072, p95 1330, p99 1723 tokens.**  
  Sampled 4,000 rows from each of the 10 instruct_*.jsonl files, tokens estimated as chars/4.0 (calibrated: this reproduces the meta.yaml mean of 417 tokens exactly). Per-source >1024 rates: slimorca 2.4%, ultrachat 48.8%, wizardlm 4.7%, metamath 0.0%, alpaca 0.0%, hh_helpful 0.1%, oasst 1.1%, capybara 50.0%, dolly 1.6%, no_robots 2.0%.

- **Naive concatenate-and-chunk packing is quantified as harmful in the literature. Best-fit Packing (which eliminates unnecessary truncation) yields +4.7% reading comprehension, +16.8% context following, +9.2% program synthesis, and up to 58.3% reduction in closed-domain hallucination versus naive concatenation.**  
  'Fewer Truncations Improve Language Modeling' (ICML 2024): naive concatenation 'inevitably breaks many documents into incomplete pieces, leading to excessive truncations' and 'hinders models from learning to compose logically coherent and factually consistent content grounded on the complete context'. Best-fit Packing has the same compute cost as naive concatenation.  
  _src: https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.10830_
- **Cross-document attention inside a packed row is a separate, additive harm. Packing without example-boundary awareness causes 'undesired cross-example attention that reduce quality and convergence'. Managing pack composition/contamination is worth up to 7% on GSM8K and 4% on HumanEval.**  
  IBM/HuggingFace: 'previous implementations of packing did not consider example boundaries when using Flash Attention 2, resulting in undesired cross-example attention that reduce quality and convergence'; fix is position_ids -> block-diagonal attention (DataCollatorWithFlattening in Trainer, padding_free=True in TRL), giving 2x throughput on FLAN and 1.4x on OrcaMath. Threshold Filtering Packing reports 'up to 7% improvement on GSM8K and 4% on HumanEval' and states 'attention cross-contamination lowers the model's performance on complex reasoning tasks'.  
  _src: https://research.ibm.com/blog/hugging-face-training-flash-attention , https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.09327_
- **Plasma's attention has no mechanism for document boundaries: src/model/attention.py sets `is_causal = mask is None and kv_cache is None` and the training path passes mask=None, so every token attends to every earlier token in the 1024-window regardless of which conversation it belongs to. RoPE positions also run 0..1023 across the whole row rather than restarting per conversation.**  
  src/model/attention.py lines 59-73: `attn_mask = mask; is_causal = mask is None and kv_cache is None; ... F.scaled_dot_product_attention(q, k, v, attn_mask=attn_mask, ..., is_causal=is_causal)`. src/train/train.py calls `logits = model(x)` with no mask argument.  
  _src: C:\Users\eb\Downloads\1386.ai\src\model\attention.py, C:\Users\eb\Downloads\1386.ai\src\train\train.py_
- **BUG: the masked-loss normalization in src/train/train.py is the exact HuggingFace/Tulu-3 gradient-accumulation bug. Loss is normalized per micro-batch by that micro-batch's own assistant-token count, then divided by grad_accum. With micro_batch=2 and grad_accum=16, each micro-batch is weighted equally instead of each token being weighted equally, so short-answer micro-batches (e.g. MetaMath/Alpaca, median 166/69 tokens) get several times more gradient weight per token than long ones.**  
  src/train/train.py: `masked_loss = (loss_per_token * mask_flat).sum() / (mask_flat.sum() + 1e-8); loss = masked_loss / grad_accum`. Tulu 3: 'we opted to use a sum loss instead of averaging (mean loss) when training, which removes the issue by removing the denominator... effectively weighting all tokens equally which led to generally better performance'. Tulu 3 8B card lists 'Loss Accumulation: Sum'. HF issue #24725 and the Oct-2024 gradient-accumulation bug writeups describe the same failure mode.  
  _src: C:\Users\eb\Downloads\1386.ai\src\train\train.py , https://huggingface.co/allenai/Llama-3.1-Tulu-3-8B-SFT , https://github.com/huggingface/transformers/issues/24725 , https://unsloth.ai/blog/gradient_
- **LIKELY BUG (needs a 3-line verification): scripts/build_sft_v2.py tokenizes each turn segment separately - `sp.encode(text[role_start:label_end])` for 'Assistant: ' (including the trailing space, since ROLE_RE ends in ': ?') and then `sp.encode(text[label_end:next_start])` for the content. With SentencePiece's default add_dummy_prefix, the label segment ends in a '_' piece and the content segment starts with '_The', producing a doubled space token at every single turn boundary that never occurs at inference, where chat.py builds the prompt as one joint string ending in 'Assistant:'.**  
  build_sft_v2.py: `ROLE_RE = re.compile(r"(?:^|\n)(User|Assistant): ?")`; `ltoks = sp.encode(text[role_start:label_end], out_type=int)` then `ctoks = sp.encode(text[label_end:next_start], out_type=int)`. src/inference/chat.py line 25: `return f"User: {last_user}\nAssistant:"` - joint encode, no trailing space. Tokenizer is trained with `normalization_rule_name='identity'`, byte_fallback, default add_dummy_prefix.

- **LIMA's '1,000 examples is enough' does NOT transfer to a 521M model or to benchmark-style evaluation. It was a 65B model judged by human preference on open-ended generation. When you measure with objective task benchmarks instead, post-training performance follows a power law P ~ D^(1/b) in the number of finetuning examples across Llama-3, Llama-2 and Mistral: style saturates at 100-500 examples, but reasoning errors keep dropping substantially through 10,000+ examples.**  
  LIMA: 65B LLaMa, 1,000 prompts; ablation scaling Stack Exchange 2k/4k/8k/16k/32k found 'doubling the training set does not improve response quality'. 'Revisiting the Superficial Alignment Hypothesis': task-specific finetuning on 1,000 examples gives 46.5% GSM8k while LIMA-1k gives 14.7% GSM8k despite an 84.4% human win-rate; 'stylistic improvements saturated quickly (around 100 to 500 examples)' whereas 'reasoning errors continued to decrease substantially with additional finetuning data'; r^2 > 0.98 correlation between total mistakes and reasoning errors.  
  _src: https://ar5iv.labs.arxiv.org/html/2305.11206 , https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.03717 , https://www.alphaxiv.org/overview/2410.03717_

### Recommendations
- [high] **Raise SFT peak LR from 1.0e-5 to 8.0e-5, cosine decay to min_lr 8.0e-6 (10% of peak), keeping batch_size 32 / micro_batch 2 / grad_accum 16 (32,768 tokens per optimizer step). In configs/finetune_1.1_v2.yaml: learning_rate: 8.0e-5, min_lr: 8.0e-6. Also set max_grad_norm: 1.0 (from 0.5) and weight_decay: 0.0 (from 0.05).**  
  why: Three independent anchors converge on 5e-5 to 1.2e-4 for a 521M model at 32,768 tokens/step. (1) SmolTulu ran the identical base model and data at 9.0e-5 with the same tokens-per-step and beat a 3.1e-6 run by +13.2 IFEval and +6.2 GSM8K. (2) Scaling SmolLM2-360M's 1.0e-3 @ 1,048,576 tokens/step down to Plasma's 32,768 tokens/step gives 3.1e-5 (linear) to 1.77e-4 (sqrt); a 521M model wants the upper half of that vs a 360M model's data point. (3) SmolLM2's SFT LR is 0.3-0.6x its pretrain peak LR; 0.6 x 1.2e-4 = 7.2e-5. max_grad_norm 0.5 will bind on nearly every step at this LR and silently re-impose a lower effective LR; 1.0 matches the pretrain config. Tulu-3/open-instruct use weight_decay 0.0 for SFT.  
  gain: The single largest expected win. SmolTulu's controlled comparison is +13.2 IFEval / +6.2 GSM8K from LR alone on the same base and data. On Plasma's own honest benchmark, expect the biggest movement on instruction following and format adherence, which are currently the cheapest points available given 1.0 = 43% and old 1.1 = 41%.  
  risk: Too-high LR on a tied 48000x1280 embedding (61.4M params, 11.8% of the model) can wash out pretrained features; watch for training loss that drops fast then plateaus above the 1e-5 run's loss, or grad_norm consistently >2.0. Mitigation: log grad_norm every 50 steps and abort if the held-out assistant-token loss at step 1000 is worse than the 1e-5 baseline. No VRAM impact.  
  verdict: (unverified) — 
- [high] **Before committing 10 hours, run a 3-point LR sweep of one epoch each (5,268 steps, ~3.5 h each at 2.4 s/step) at 4e-5, 8e-5, 1.5e-4. Select on (a) held-out assistant-token cross-entropy and (b) the existing scripts/test_20_prompts.py / eval_prompts.py suite plus a new EOS-rate metric. Total cost ~10.5 h.**  
  why: SmolTulu's entire contribution is that the optimal LR/batch ratio for sub-2B models is not extrapolable from 7B-70B recipes, and their two runs differ by more than any data change in this report. A one-epoch signal is enough to rank three LRs and is cheaper than one bad three-epoch run.  
  gain: De-risks the single highest-variance hyperparameter. Removes the guesswork between 4e-5 and 1.5e-4.  
  risk: Held-out loss can rank LRs differently from downstream benchmarks (lower LR usually wins on loss, higher LR usually wins on IFEval-style tasks). Break ties with the prompt suite, not the loss.  
  verdict: (unverified) — 
- [high] **Replace 'wrapped' packing in scripts/build_sft_v2.py with best-fit-decreasing (BFD) packing that never splits a conversation. Sort conversations by length descending, greedily place each into the fullest bin that still fits within 1024, pad the remainder with pad_id=3 (mask 0). For the 11.1% of conversations over 1024 tokens, truncate at the last complete assistant turn that fits (keeping its EOS) rather than mid-cut; drop only if no complete turn fits.**  
  why: MEASURED: 40.6% of Plasma's 423,235 conversations are currently split across a row boundary (171,977 boundaries / 423,235 conversations, mean length 417 vs row length 1025). Every split trains the model on an assistant answer with no question in context and on an answer that ends without EOS - a textbook cause of rambling and non-termination. Best-fit Packing reports +16.8% context following, +9.2% program synthesis, and up to 58.3% less closed-domain hallucination purely from eliminating truncation. TRL made BFD the default packing strategy in 0.19.0 and documents 'wrapped' as 'aggressive, cuts mid-sequence'.  
  gain: Largest data-side win. Fixes 172k damaged training examples. Expect the clearest effect on stopping behaviour and multi-step reasoning (currently ~0%). Padding overhead with BFD at median 257 / mean 417 into 1024-token bins is typically <5%, so tokens/epoch stays roughly 176M -> ~185M rows-worth.  
  risk: Slightly more rows for the same content (padding), so recompute steps/epoch from the new n_train_sequences. Truncating long UltraChat/Capybara conversations at a turn boundary discards ~11% of raw tokens; acceptable, and better than the current mid-answer cuts. No VRAM impact.  
  verdict: (unverified) — 
- [medium] **Add per-document block-diagonal attention and per-document RoPE positions. Emit a `doc_id` uint8/uint16 array alongside tokens and masks in the shards. In src/model/attention.py, build `mask = (doc_id[:,None,:,None] == doc_id[:,None,None,:]) & causal_lower_tri` once per forward (shape B,1,S,S bool) and pass it into F.scaled_dot_product_attention with is_causal=False. In src/model/rope.py, index freqs_cis by per-document positions (restart at 0 at each document start) instead of arange(S).**  
  why: Plasma currently sets `is_causal = mask is None` with mask=None during training, so a token in conversation 3 of a row attends to conversations 1 and 2. IBM/HF state this 'reduce[s] quality and convergence'; Threshold Filtering Packing measures up to 7% GSM8K and 4% HumanEval attributable to how packs are composed. This is the same fix TRL applies automatically via padding_free / position_ids.  
  gain: Secondary to the truncation fix but additive; literature range 2-7% on reasoning and code benchmarks. Also makes the SFT distribution match single-conversation inference exactly.  
  risk: Passing an explicit attn_mask to PyTorch SDPA disables the flash kernel and falls back to the memory-efficient/math backend. At B=2, S=1024, 20 heads, head_dim 64 with gradient checkpointing the transient cost is bounded (~2MB for the bool mask; worst-case math-backend scores are ~168MB fp32 for a single layer at a time), so it fits in 16GB, but expect a 15-30% throughput hit (2.4 s/step -> ~2.8-3.1 s/step). If the slowdown is unacceptable, ship the BFD fix alone - it captures the larger share of the benefit. Test with torch.backends.cuda.sdp_kernel to confirm which backend is selected.  
  verdict: (unverified) — 
- [high] **Fix the loss normalization in src/train/train.py. Replace the per-micro-batch mean with a fixed token-count normalizer: `NORM = micro_batch_size * seq_len * grad_accum * assistant_token_frac` (currently 2 * 1024 * 16 * 0.668 = 21,893, read assistant_token_frac from meta.yaml) and compute `loss = (loss_per_token * mask_flat).sum() / NORM` with NO further division by grad_accum. Or, exactly correct: prefetch the grad_accum micro-batches, compute total assistant tokens for the step, and divide by that.**  
  why: The current `(lpt*mask).sum() / mask.sum() / grad_accum` weights each micro-batch equally rather than each token equally - the exact bug Tulu 3 identified and solved with sum loss ('effectively weighting all tokens equally which led to generally better performance'), and the same one HF/Unsloth documented as the gradient-accumulation bug. With micro_batch=2 and a mixture spanning median lengths from 69 (Alpaca) to 1014 (UltraChat), per-token gradient weight varies by several times across micro-batches. The fixed normalizer 21,893 is within 0.03% of the current effective normalizer (16 x 2 x 1024 x 0.668 = 21,888), so gradient magnitude is preserved and the LR does NOT need retuning.  
  gain: Removes a systematic bias that over-weights short-answer sources (MetaMath, Alpaca) relative to long-answer sources (UltraChat, Capybara). Tulu 3 called it 'critical for consistent performance'. No throughput or VRAM cost.  
  risk: If you switch to a pure sum loss (NORM=1) instead, the LR must be re-scaled by ~1/21,893 - do not do that accidentally. Verify by confirming the logged loss value stays in the same numeric range as before the change.  
  verdict: (unverified) — 
- [high] **Retrain the tokenizer with ChatML control tokens reserved inside the existing 48,000 vocab: add `user_defined_symbols=["<|im_start|>", "<|im_end|>"]` to spm.SentencePieceTrainer.train in scripts/train_tokenizer.py. SentencePiece counts user_defined_symbols against vocab_size, so vocab stays exactly 48000 (still divisible by 64, no tensor-core padding change, no model config change). Format conversations as `<|im_start|>user\n{content}<|im_end|>\n<|im_start|>assistant\n{content}<|im_end|>` and stop generation on <|im_end|>.**  
  why: Every reference small-model recipe uses dedicated control tokens: SmolLM2-135M/360M use <|im_start|>/<|im_end|>, Tulu 3 uses <|user|>/<|assistant|>/<|endoftext|>. Transformers docs require them to be registered as special so 'they are never split... always handled as single tokens'. Plain 'User:/Assistant:' tokenizes into 3-5 pieces per turn marker, is ambiguous with body text that quotes dialogue, and provides no unambiguous stop symbol - which is why src/inference/chat.py needs a fragile string-stop list `['\nUser:', '\nSystem:', '\nHuman:', '\nQuestion:', '\n\n\n']`. LIMA's rationale applies directly: a dedicated turn terminator 'avoids conflation with any other meaning that the pretrained model may have imbued into the preexisting EOS token'.  
  gain: Cleaner and more reliable stopping, ~4-6 fewer tokens per turn marker (a real saving at 1024 context), and elimination of the string-matching stop heuristics. Also removes the tokenization-boundary artifact described below.  
  risk: Requires a tokenizer retrain and therefore a pretrain data re-shard. If pretraining is already >30% underway, do NOT restart - use the fallback below instead. If pretraining has not started, this is free.  
  verdict: (unverified) — 
- [medium] **FALLBACK if the tokenizer cannot be retrained: keep the plain-text template but fix the segmented-tokenization boundary. Stop calling sp.encode() separately per turn segment. Tokenize the whole conversation once with `sp.encode(text, out_type='immutable_proto')` and derive the assistant loss mask from each piece's `begin`/`end` character offsets. Verify the current defect first with: `sp.encode('Assistant: ') + sp.encode('The three primary colors...')` vs `sp.encode('Assistant: The three primary colors...')`.**  
  why: scripts/build_sft_v2.py encodes the label span 'Assistant: ' (ROLE_RE ends in ': ?', so the trailing space is inside the label span) separately from the content. With SentencePiece's default add_dummy_prefix, the label segment likely ends in a '_' piece and the content segment starts with '_The', injecting a doubled-space token at EVERY turn boundary that never occurs at inference - src/inference/chat.py builds 'User: ...\nAssistant:' as a single joint string with no trailing space. This is a train/inference mismatch on the very first supervised token of every response, in 100% of examples.  
  gain: If the defect is confirmed, fixing it removes a systematic mismatch on the first token of every assistant turn - a common cause of responses that begin with a stray space or an off-distribution first token. Offset-based masking is also more robust than regex spans.  
  risk: Must be verified before acting - if SentencePiece collapses the trailing space, there is no bug and no change is needed. The verification is three lines and costs nothing. Note the regex itself is fine: measured 0.0-0.3% of conversations have non-alternating User/Assistant markers, so mis-parsing is not a material problem.  
  verdict: (unverified) — 
- [high] **Grow the SFT set from 423k conversations / 176M tokens to roughly 500-600k conversations / 250-280M tokens, and rebalance toward the SmolTalk/Tulu-3 shape. Target: general chat 40% (SlimOrca 100k, UltraChat 70k, OpenHermes-2.5 60k, Magpie-Pro-300K-Filtered 60k), math 17% (MetaMathQA 45k + NuminaMath-CoT 40k), CODE 12% (Evol-CodeAlpaca-v1 45k + self-oss-instruct-sc2/Self-OSS-Starcoder2-Instruct 30k), instruction-following/constraints 7% (smol-constraints 36k), short writing 8% (smol-summarize 25k + smol-rewrite 20k), system prompts 4% (SystemChats-2.0 20k), human-written 8% (OASST 20k, Dolly 15k, No-Robots 9.5k), identity 240 x 8 replicas. Cut hh_helpful from 30k to 10k. Cap any single source at 20%.**  
  why: Plasma has ZERO dedicated code data, which exactly matches the reported ~0% on executable code; SmolTalk allocates 4.6% and Tulu 3 allocates 15.1% to code. It also has zero constraint-following data (smol-constraints is decontaminated against IFEval), zero summarization/rewriting, and zero system-prompt data - three of the five named goals. HuggingFace chose 485k rows for their 135M/360M models, not 1k. 'Revisiting the Superficial Alignment Hypothesis' shows reasoning follows a power law in example count and only style saturates at 100-500 examples, so the LIMA 1k result does not apply to Plasma's benchmark. hh_helpful is preference data repurposed as SFT and is the weakest per-token signal in the current mix.  
  gain: Code is the only path from 0% to non-zero on executable code - there is currently no code supervision at all. Constraint data is the direct lever on instruction following. Expect the largest single-category movement here of any change in this report.  
  risk: More data means more steps (250M tokens / 32,768 = 7,630 steps/epoch, 2 epochs = 15,260 steps = ~10.2 h at 2.4 s/step). Magpie and NuminaMath are distilled from larger models, so decontaminate against eval_prompts.py exactly as build_sft_v2.py already does (it dropped only 40 rows - widen the check to n-gram overlap, not exact prompt match). Some sources carry non-commercial licenses (No-Robots CC-BY-NC-4.0). No VRAM impact.  
  verdict: (unverified) — 
- [medium] **Train 2 epochs, not 3, with a single cosine schedule spanning the full run and warmup at 3% of total steps. On the current 168,539-row set: 5,268 steps/epoch, max_steps 10,536, warmup_steps 320. On the expanded ~250M-token set: ~7,630 steps/epoch, max_steps 15,260, warmup_steps 460. Checkpoint every 1,300 steps (~0.25 epoch) and select on held-out assistant-token loss plus the prompt suite.**  
  why: SmolLM2 uses 2 epochs on 485k/1.1M rows; Tulu 3 uses 2 epochs on 939,344 rows; SmolTalk's own card specifies 2 epochs. LIMA's 15 epochs applies to 1,030 examples. Plasma's 3-epoch plan (max_steps 16000 at eff batch 32) is not obviously wrong for a base trained on only ~10.5B tokens, but 2 epochs is the documented consensus and it frees 3.5 h. Setting cosine over the full 2 epochs avoids the trap of stopping mid-decay at a high LR. Note the existing warmup_steps of 500 is 4.7% - close, but 3% matches Tulu 3's warmup_ratio 0.03.  
  gain: Removes ~3.5 h of compute and one epoch of memorization risk with no documented downside at this data scale. Frees budget for the LR sweep.  
  risk: If the expanded mixture is used and the eval curve is still improving at 2.0 epochs, a 3rd epoch may help - the power-law result argues against early saturation for reasoning. Decide from the per-quarter-epoch checkpoints, not a priori.  
  verdict: (unverified) — 
- [medium] **Keep assistant-only loss masking (use_loss_mask: true) as the primary setting, but keep the assistant role-marker tokens (<|im_start|>assistant\n, or 'Assistant: ' in the fallback) IN the loss rather than masking them to 0 as build_mask currently does, and always keep the final <|im_end|>/EOS in the loss (already correct).**  
  why: 'Instruction Tuning With Loss Over Instructions' shows full-sequence loss wins mainly below ~15,000 examples and at high instruction:output ratio; Plasma has 423k+ conversations at assistant_token_frac 0.668 (ratio ~0.5), squarely in the regime where completion-only is the right default - matching Tulu 3. Supervising the assistant turn marker costs almost nothing and teaches the model to produce well-formed turn structure when it is continuing a multi-turn conversation. Note the honest counter-evidence: SmolLM2-135M/360M-Instruct were trained with loss on all tokens (TRL defaults assistant_only_loss=False, completion_only_loss=None), so this is not settled at 500M.  
  gain: Small but free. The main value is knowing the decision is evidence-backed rather than assumed.  
  risk: Because SmolLM2 at exactly this scale used full-sequence loss, a one-epoch ablation with use_loss_mask=false is worth 3.5 h if budget allows. Masking currently discards 33.2% of tokens' gradient signal from a base that has only seen ~10.5B pretraining tokens, which is the argument on the other side.  
  verdict: (unverified) — 
- [high] **Make stopping a first-class, measured behaviour. (1) Report '% of held-out generations that terminate on <|im_end|>/EOS within 512 tokens' after every eval, alongside loss. (2) Inject 3,000-5,000 very short assistant answers ('Paris.', '42', 'Yes, because...') into the mixture so EOS is observed at low token indices. (3) Keep pad_id=3 strictly distinct from eos_id=2 and confirm pad positions always carry mask 0 in the new BFD padding. (4) At inference, stop on <|im_end|> OR eos_id.**  
  why: Plasma already appends EOS with mask=1 - the right call - but 40.6% of conversations are split by wrapped packing, so a large fraction of trained EOS positions follow a fragment that began in a different row, and an equal number of row-heads have no EOS at all. Additionally, with mean conversation length 417 tokens, EOS is almost never seen early, so the model builds a length prior. The pad==eos trap (the most common cause of models that never stop) is already avoided by the pad_id=3/eos_id=2 split - do not let BFD padding reintroduce it.  
  gain: Directly addresses non-termination, which compounds with every generative benchmark. A measured EOS rate turns an invisible failure into a tracked number.  
  risk: Over-injecting short answers can bias the model toward terse responses; cap at ~1% of conversations. Ensure the short answers are correct and diverse (No-Robots and Dolly already contain many).  
  verdict: (unverified) — 
- [high] **Do NOT apply DEITA/AlpaGasus/IFD/InsTag-style aggressive selection (top 5-10%, or 6k-9k curated sets). Keep the cheap quality filters already in build_sft_v2.py (BAD_PATTERNS refusal/AI-disclaimer removal, len<24 drop, benchmark decontamination) and add: drop responses with truncated final sentences, drop responses under 3 tokens unless the question is closed-form, and dedupe near-identical prompts by 5-gram Jaccard > 0.8.**  
  why: DEITA (6k), AlpaGasus (9k of 52k), Cherry/IFD (5-10%) and InsTag (6k) were all validated at 7B-13B on MT-Bench/AlpacaEval - the chat-style axis that 'Revisiting the Superficial Alignment Hypothesis' shows saturates at 100-500 examples. Plasma's benchmark is factual QA, code, arithmetic and multi-step reasoning, which follow a power law in example count. The realistic ceiling for curation at this scale is the TuluTalk result: 14% fewer samples at equal performance - useful, not transformative.  
  gain: Avoids a large regression. Light filtering plus dedup typically removes 5-15% of rows at no cost to benchmarks.  
  risk: The dedup threshold matters: Alpaca and MetaMath are intentionally templated and near-duplicate by construction; apply dedup within-source with a loose threshold rather than globally, or you will delete most of MetaMath.  
  verdict: (unverified) — 
- [medium] **Skip NEFTune for the main run. If you want a chat-feel variant later, use noise_alpha=5 (per-element magnitude 5/sqrt(1024*1280) = 0.00437 for Plasma's d=1280, L=1024) as a separate 1-epoch fork and evaluate it separately - and pair it with repetition_penalty 1.2 at generation as the authors specify.**
